⏺ ---
  Summary of v0

  m3-sys/msir/ package created and working:
  - src/MSIR.i3 (~150 lines) — typed-SSA IR public API: types, values, modules,
  procedures, blocks, instructions, builders.
  - src/MSIR.m3 (~400 lines) — implementation with branded opaque types,
  RefSeq-backed collections, automatic SSA name generation.
  - src/MSIRPrinter.i3 / .m3 (~250 lines) — textual printer matching the
  walkthrough syntax.
  - test/src/Main.m3 — smoke test that builds Sum0ToN programmatically and
  prints it.
  - m3makefiles for both library and test.

  v0 scope (intentionally bounded):
  - Types: void, signed/unsigned int widths, floats, ptr, gc_ref, gc_slot,
  struct, proc.
  - Values: const int/bool/nil, parameters, block parameters, instruction
  results.
  - Ops: load, store, iadd, isub, imul, icmp, br, cond_br, ret, unreachable,
  call.

  Deferred to next commits (each is a localized addition, not architectural):
  - Object types (with super, fields, methods, descriptor) — needed for the
  OBJECT walkthrough.
  - Open-array types and ops — needed for the open-array walkthrough.
  - EH envelopes (try, try_finally, raise, unwind_to, ret_through_envelope) —
  needed for the TRY walkthrough.
  - M3 intrinsics (dispatch, narrow, istype, typecase, range_check,
  subscript_check, nil_check, set_*).
  - Allocas, gc.load/store, field_addr, new.
  - A verifier (D8 cross-envelope rule, type consistency, dominance, etc.).
  - A parser for round-tripping textual MSIR.

  The shape is now concrete — every future walkthrough can typecheck against
  this package, growing the IR opcode by opcode without re-litigating the
  architectural decisions in MSIR-design.md. Future-me reading the smoke test
  output knows exactly what the IR looks like in code form, not just in
  walkthrough text.

✻ Worked for 10m 15s

❯ Before we go on, consider the following and give me your thoughts: Great      
  question — this word is doing *a lot* of work in the design, so it’s worth    
  being very precise.                                                           
                                                                                
  When I say **“structured”** (in *structured semantic IR* like MSIR), I mean:  
                                                                                
  > **Control flow and program constructs are represented using high‑level,     
  nested language constructs — not flattened into jumps, basic blocks, or graph 
   edges.**                                                                     
                                                                                
  Let’s unpack that concretely.                                                 
                                                                                
  ***                                                                           
                                                                                
  # 1. Structured vs unstructured (the core distinction)                        
                                                                                
  ## ✅ Structured IR (what MSIR should be)                                     
                                                                                
  Control flow is expressed using **nested, tree‑shaped constructs**:           
                                                                                
  ```text                                                                       
  IF cond THEN                                                                  
    ...                                                                         
  ELSE                                                                          
    ...                                                                         
  END                                                                           
                                                                                
  TYPECASE x OF                                                                 
  | ref A AS a => ...                                                           
  | ref B AS b => ...                                                           
  END                                                                           
                                                                                
  WHILE cond DO                                                                 
    ...                                                                         
  END                                                                           
  ```                                                                           
                                                                                
  Characteristics:                                                              
                                                                                
  *   Single entry / single exit per construct                                  
  *   Nesting is explicit (like an AST)                                         
  *   Control flow is *implied* by the structure                                
  *   No explicit jumps                                                         
                                                                                
  ***                                                                           
                                                                                
  ## ❌ Unstructured IR (what LLVM IR is)                                       
                                                                                
  Control flow is reduced to **basic blocks and branches**:                     
                                                                                
  ```text                                                                       
  block1:                                                                       
    br cond, block2, block3                                                     
                                                                                
  block2:                                                                       
    ...                                                                         
    br block4                                                                   
                                                                                
  block3:                                                                       
    ...                                                                         
    br block4                                                                   
                                                                                
  block4:                                                                       
    ...                                                                         
  ```                                                                           
                                                                                
  Characteristics:                                                              
                                                                                
  *   Graph, not tree                                                           
  *   Arbitrary jumps                                                           
  *   Requires φ-nodes to merge values                                          
  *   Structure is lost and must be rediscovered (if at all)                    
                                                                                
  ***                                                                           
                                                                                
  # 2. Why “structured” matters specifically for MSIR                           
                                                                                
  MSIR is your **semantic IR**, so it needs to preserve:                        
                                                                                
  ### 2.1 Language meaning                                                      
                                                                                
  Example: TYPECASE                                                             
                                                                                
  ```text                                                                       
  TYPECASE x OF                                                                 
  | ref A AS a => ...                                                           
  | ref B AS b => ...                                                           
  END                                                                           
  ```                                                                           
                                                                                
  In structured MSIR:                                                           
                                                                                
  *   You know this is a **type dispatch construct**                            
  *   You can enforce:                                                          
      *   disjointness                                                          
      *   exhaustiveness                                                        
      *   correct binding of `a`, `b`                                           
                                                                                
  In unstructured form:                                                         
                                                                                
  *   It becomes a sequence of comparisons and branches                         
  *   The compiler must *reconstruct intent*                                    
  *   Safety checks become extremely fragile                                    
                                                                                
  ***                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### 2.2 Exception regions                                                     
                                                                                
  Structured:                                                                   
                                                                                
  ```text                                                                       
  TRY                                                                           
    ...                                                                         
  EXCEPT E => ...                                                               
  END                                                                           
  ```                                                                           
                                                                                
  Unstructured (after lowering):                                                
                                                                                
  *   multiple blocks                                                           
  *   landingpads                                                               
  *   hidden control transfers                                                  
                                                                                
  In structured MSIR:                                                           
                                                                                
  *   exception regions are explicit                                            
  *   correctness is checkable                                                  
                                                                                
  ***                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### 2.3 GC safety points                                                      
                                                                                
  Structured MSIR makes it clear:                                               
                                                                                
  *   where objects live                                                        
  *   where control merges                                                      
  *   where roots must exist                                                    
                                                                                
  In unstructured SSA:                                                          
                                                                                
  *   lifetimes are fragmented across blocks                                    
  *   GC errors become subtle                                                   
                                                                                
  ***                                                                           
                                                                                
  # 3. Structured ≠ AST (important distinction)                                 
                                                                                
  MSIR is **structured**, but not just a syntax tree.                           
                                                                                
  ### AST example                                                               
                                                                                
  ```text                                                                       
  if (x + 1 > 0) ...                                                            
  ```                                                                           
                                                                                
  AST cares about:                                                              
                                                                                
  *   operator precedence                                                       
  *   syntax                                                                    
                                                                                
  ***                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### MSIR structured form                                                      
                                                                                
  ```text                                                                       
  v1 = add(x, 1)                                                                
  v2 = gt(v1, 0)                                                                
                                                                                
  IF v2 THEN ...                                                                
  ```                                                                           
                                                                                
  So MSIR is:                                                                   
                                                                                
  *   **structured control flow**                                               
  *   *   **lowered expressions (SSA-like values)**                             
                                                                                
  Think:                                                                        
                                                                                
  > **AST = syntax tree**                                                       
  > **MSIR = semantic tree with explicit evaluation**                           
                                                                                
  ***                                                                           
                                                                                
  # 4. Visual intuition                                                         
                                                                                
  ## Structured MSIR (tree)                                                     
                                                                                
  ```text                                                                       
  Proc                                                                          
   ├─ IF                                                                        
   │   ├─ cond: v                                                               
   │   ├─ then:                                                                 
   │   │   └─ ...                                                               
   │   └─ else:                                                                 
   │       └─ ...                                                               
   └─ RETURN                                                                    
  ```                                                                           
                                                                                
  ***                                                                           
                                                                                
  ## Unstructured LLVM (graph)                                                  
                                                                                
  ```text                                                                       
     [entry]                                                                    
      /   \                                                                     
   [b1]  [b2]                                                                   
      \   /                                                                     
      [merge]                                                                   
  ```                                                                           
                                                                                
  MSIR is a **tree of regions**                                                 
  LLVM is a **graph of blocks**                                                 
                                                                                
  ***                                                                           
                                                                                
  # 5. Why this is critical for your design                                     
                                                                                
  Because your entire plan relies on:                                           
                                                                                
  ### ✅ Doing correctness *before* lowering                                    
                                                                                
  You check:                                                                    
                                                                                
  *   TYPECASE exhaustiveness                                                   
  *   narrowing safety                                                          
  *   opaque type rules                                                         
  *   GC trace completeness                                                     
                                                                                
  These are all **easily checkable in structured form**.                        
                                                                                
  ***                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### ❌ If you go unstructured too early                                       
                                                                                
  You would:                                                                    
                                                                                
  *   lose TYPECASE semantics                                                   
  *   lose opacity guarantees                                                   
  *   need dataflow analysis to rediscover meaning                              
  *   reintroduce the exact problems M3CG has                                   
                                                                                
  ***                                                                           
                                                                                
  # 6. Where structure ends (important boundary)                                
                                                                                
  Structure is preserved **until**:                                             
                                                                                
      MSIR → LLVM lowering                                                      
                                                                                
  At that point:                                                                
                                                                                
  *   You flatten:                                                              
      *   IF → branches                                                         
      *   TYPECASE → comparisons + branches                                     
      *   loops → backedges                                                     
  *   You insert:                                                               
      *   φ nodes                                                               
      *   block labels                                                          
                                                                                
  Because:                                                                      
                                                                                
  > LLVM is designed to work this way.                                          
                                                                                
  But by then:                                                                  
                                                                                
  *   correctness is already proven                                             
  *   ambiguity is gone                                                         
                                                                                
  ***                                                                           
                                                                                
  # 7. Precise definition (useful for docs)                                     
                                                                                
  You can define it formally like this:                                         
                                                                                
  > **A structured IR is one in which control flow is expressed as a hierarchy  
  of single-entry, single-exit regions, and all control transfers occur through 
   syntactically nested constructs rather than explicit jump edges.**           
                                                                                
  That’s the property you want MSIR to satisfy.                                 
                                                                                
  ***                                                                           
                                                                                
  # 8. Quick comparison table                                                   
                                                                                
  | Property            | Structured MSIR   | LLVM IR         |                 
  | ------------------- | ----------------- | --------------- |                 
  | Control flow        | Nested constructs | Graph of blocks |                 
  | TYPECASE meaning    | Explicit          | Lost            |                 
  | Exhaustiveness      | Checkable         | Not directly    |                 
  | GC safety reasoning | Clear             | Distributed     |                 
  | SSA form            | Partial (values)  | Full            |                 
  | Debuggability       | High              | Lower-level     |                 
                                                                                
  ***                                                                           
                                                                                
  # Final takeaway                                                              
                                                                                
  > **“Structured” means MSIR preserves the *shape and meaning* of Modula‑3     
  programs — control flow is represented as nested constructs you can reason    
  about, not as arbitrary jumps you have to reverse-engineer.**                 
                                                                                
  That’s exactly what lets you:                                                 
                                                                                
  *   prove correctness early                                                   
  *   keep LLVM honest                                                          
  *   and avoid repeating the M3CG problem                                      
                                                                                
  ***                                                                           
                                                                                
  If you want next, I can:                                                      
                                                                                
  *   show a full example: Modula‑3 → MSIR (structured + SSA) → LLVM            
  *   or define a formal “region-based MSIR” model (like Swift SIL regions)     
                                                                                

⏺ This is the right architectural pushback to make and worth taking seriously.
  Let me give my honest reaction — I think the user's framing has a real insight
   underneath, but the conclusion (full tree IR) overshoots and the supporting
  evidence for SIL/MIR is incorrect.

  Where the argument is right

  The deepest point — language meaning must survive into the IR or you get M3CG 
  redux — is correct. If we flatten TYPECASE into a sequence of istype + cond_br
   early, exhaustiveness checks die, the dispatch intent dies, and the optimizer
   can't fold the way our walkthrough showed. Same for TRY/EXCEPT/FINALLY: the
  M3CG Start_try/End_try/Landing_pad ordering hazard is exactly what you get
  when you flatten a structured EH region into ordered linear ops.

  That insight is load-bearing. It's why our existing design has:

  - Composite ops for everything M3-distinctive: dispatch, narrow, istype,
  typecase (with parameterized clause blocks), subarray, range_check,
  subscript_check, set_*, gc.load, gc.store, new.
  - Structured envelopes for EH: try { … } except { clauses }, try_finally { … }
   finally { … }, with explicit non-local exit ops (unwind_to,
  ret_through_envelope) that the verifier requires for any cross-envelope
  control transfer.
  - !invariant.load / addrspace(1) / nocapture preserving M3 facts at the LLVM
  boundary.

  So we already preserve language meaning where it lives — at the level of
  Modula-3 constructs that have semantic invariants worth checking.

  Where the argument overshoots

  The leap from "preserve language meaning" to "all control flow must be
  tree-shaped" is the part I'd push back on. Three reasons.

  1. The SIL/MIR analogy is wrong. Both Swift SIL and Rust MIR are CFGs with 
  basic blocks and block arguments — exactly the shape we have. They're not tree
   IRs. SIL has try_apply with normal/error successor blocks, switch_enum
  branching to parameterized blocks, etc.; MIR has Terminator::SwitchInt,
  Terminator::Drop, etc., all of which are CFG terminators. What makes those IRs
   semantic isn't their control-flow shape — it's their typed values and their
  high-level composite operations. The exhaustiveness check on a SIL switch_enum
   is on the op, not on the surrounding tree, because there is no surrounding
  tree. Same for MIR.

  The genuinely tree-shaped IRs are GHC's STG, WebAssembly text, and MLIR's
  high-level dialects (which lower to CFG before optimization). MLIR is the most
   relevant example, and MLIR explicitly says: use structured ops where language
   meaning matters, lower to CFG before optimization runs. That's what we're
  doing.

  2. The benefits the user lists are op-level, not region-level. Walk through
  them:

  - TYPECASE exhaustiveness: checkable on the typecase op's clause list. The CFG
   around it doesn't matter.
  - Narrowing safety: a dataflow property over static types. Tree-walking and
  CFG-walking are equally easy; the standard is the latter.
  - GC trace completeness: a property of the type system + addrspace(1)
  discipline + gc.load/gc.store ops. Nothing region-shaped about it.
  - Opacity rules: type-system property. Types are types.

  In every case the safety property lives on the op or on the type, not on the
  control-flow shape. Making the surrounding control flow tree-shaped doesn't
  tighten any of these checks — it just makes them harder to write.

  3. Modula-3 has non-local control that fights a tree. EXIT from LOOP through
  enclosing TRY/FINALLY. RETURN from inside nested loops. RAISE propagating
  through arbitrarily many enclosing scopes. A pure tree IR has to encode all of
   these as "structured exits naming an outer region" — which is exactly what
  unwind_to label, args and ret_through_envelope value are in our design. So
  even the tree purist needs the same escape hatches; they just hide the
  CFG-ness inside the "structured" exits. We've made it explicit instead.

  The cost of going full-tree in exchange for these things: every analysis pass
  walks the tree by structural recursion, no shared infrastructure with the LLVM
   passes that follow, and the impedance mismatch at the lowering boundary is
  large rather than small.

  Where I'd refine the design in light of this

  The user's argument does highlight one risk in our v0 spine doc: I have not
  been explicit enough that the composite ops (typecase, try envelopes, etc.)
  are themselves load-bearing for the safety properties. Someone reading the
  spine doc might think "MSIR is just CFG with M3-flavored opcodes, the verifier
   is an afterthought." That's wrong, and I should fix it.

  Two concrete refinements I'd make:

  1. Add a verifier section to MSIR-design.md that names the
  structured-on-op-level invariants explicitly: TYPECASE clauses must be
  exhaustive (or have ELSE); try envelopes must have at least one handler or be
  try_finally; narrow requires the result type to be a subtype of the operand's
  MSIR static type or carry a runtime-checked widening; gc.load's operand must
  be a gc_slot; cross-envelope branches must use unwind_to. None of these are
  CFG-shape properties; they're op- or type-level.
  2. Acknowledge that a "structured view" is a useful verifier projection. When
  the verifier wants to check exhaustiveness in the presence of nested
  typecases, or narrowing-safety across nested ifs, it can compute a structured
  projection of the CFG (LLVM/MLIR call this "raise to structured control flow")
   on the fly. The IR stores the CFG; the verifier sees structure when it needs
  to. This gets the user's correctness wins without paying the optimization
  impedance cost.

  The genuinely contested middle: ordinary IF/WHILE/FOR

  There's one place reasonable people disagree: should ordinary IF/WHILE/FOR be
  structured ops in MSIR, or should they flatten to CFG? My current design says
  flatten; the user's argument says keep structured.

  The honest case for flatten: ordinary IF cond THEN A ELSE B END carries no
  Modula-3-specific invariant. There's no exhaustiveness, no type narrowing tied
   to the construct itself (M3 doesn't have flow-sensitive type refinement like
  Swift's if let), no GC region semantics. The only thing flattening loses is
  "this branch came from an IF in source" — a debug info concern, not a
  correctness one, and source positions on the resulting cond_br carry that
  information adequately.

  The honest case for keep structured: it's a small amount of structure to
  preserve, it makes round-tripping to source-aware tools (debuggers, static
  analyzers) easier, and at the LLVM lowering boundary it costs only a cond_br
  to flatten anyway.

  My read: the cost of structured IF/WHILE/FOR is paid by every optimization
  pass over MSIR. The benefit is mostly aesthetic for IR not destined for human
  consumption. So flatten — but carry source positions diligently so debugger
  and IDE tooling get what they need.

  Bottom line

  The user is right that we must not flatten language constructs prematurely —
  TYPECASE, TRY, dispatch, set ops, range checks, narrow, etc. all stay as
  composite ops with their semantic content intact, all the way to the LLVM
  lowering pass. The verifier checks structured invariants on those ops. None of
   this requires the IR around them to be tree-shaped.

  What we want is what SIL and MIR actually are: a typed-SSA CFG with
  semantically rich composite operations. The "structured" part is in the ops,
  not in the control flow surrounding them. The user's framing collapses these
  two senses of "structured" into one, and the tree-IR conclusion follows from
  that conflation rather than from the underlying argument.

  I'd update MSIR-design.md to make the verifier story explicit (decision-level
  entry) and to add a brief "what structure is and isn't" note so this debate
  doesn't have to be re-litigated. Want me to do that, or push back further on
  this argument first?

      187 +### A9. Verifier                                              
      188 +                                                              
      189 +The verifier checks invariants the optimizer and lowering pass
          +es                                                            
      190 +assume. It is *the* place where MSIR's safety claims are mecha
          +nized;                                                        
      191 +without it, every downstream pass would have to defend itself.
      192 +                                                              
      193 +- **Op-level structural invariants.** TYPECASE clauses must be
      194 +  exhaustive (or have ELSE); `try` envelopes must contain at l
          +east                                                          
      195 +  one handler or be `try_finally`; `narrow`'s target type must
          + permit                                                       
      196 +  the source type after runtime check; `gc.load`'s operand mus
          +t have                                                        
      197 +  type `gc_slot T`; `gc.store`'s operands must agree; `dispatc
          +h`'s                                                          
      198 +  selector must name a method present on the operand's static 
          +type                                                          
      199 +  or a supertype.                                             
      200 +- **Type consistency.** Operand types match the op's signature
          +;                                                             
      201 +  block-call argument types match the target's parameter list;
      202 +  procedure return types match `ret` operand types; address-sp
          +ace                                                           
      203 +  annotations propagate consistently through GEPs and bitcasts
          +.                                                             
      204 +- **Cross-envelope control rules** (D8). Plain `ret`/`br` may 
          +not                                                           
      205 +  cross an envelope boundary; only `ret_through_envelope` and 
      206 +  `unwind_to` may.                                            
      207 +- **SSA dominance.** Every operand use must be dominated by it
          +s def                                                         
      208 +  (or be a constant, procedure parameter, or block parameter o
          +f the                                                         
      209 +  use's containing block).                                    
      210 +- **GC contract** (D5, D6, D17). Traced types appear only in  
      211 +  permitted positions; the gcmap and addrspace(1) annotations 
          +agree;                                                        
      212 +  `addrspacecast` from 1→0 happens only at documented ABI boun
          +daries.                                                       
      213 +- **Procedure-attribute consistency** (D10, D16). Procedures w
          +ith                                                           
      214 +  `try` envelopes carry the personality attribute; calls insid
          +e                                                             
      215 +  envelopes use `invoke` iff the callee may raise; reference  
      216 +  parameters carry `nocapture`; etc.                          
      217 +                                                              
      218 +The verifier may compute a *structured projection* of the CFG 
          +on                                                            
      219 +demand (raising to structured control flow in the LLVM / MLIR 
          +sense)                                                        
      220 +for checks that are easier to phrase tree-recursively — for ex
          +ample,                                                        
      221 +checking that nested TYPECASE clauses cover the type lattice. 
          +The IR                                                        
      222 +storage form is the CFG; the structured view is a verifier    
      223 +projection, not a second storage form.                        
      224 +

⏺ Three updates landed in MSIR-design.md:

  - Terminology section right after Status — addresses the structured/tree
  question up-front so the next reader doesn't re-litigate it.
  - A9 (Verifier axis) — names the specific invariants the verifier owns:
  op-level structural checks, type consistency, cross-envelope rules, SSA
  dominance, GC contract, procedure-attribute consistency. Also notes that a
  structured-CFG projection is available on demand for checks that read better
  tree-recursively.
  - D20 (Structure in ops, not control flow) — pins the design position with
  explicit reasoning, citing SIL and MIR as precedent and naming the cost of
  full-tree IR.

  The doc now has a coherent answer to "why isn't MSIR more tree-shaped?" at
  three levels of depth (one-paragraph intro, axis-level invariant list,
  decision-level rationale), with cross-references between them. Future-me
  reading the doc cold gets the design intent without having to reconstruct it
  from walkthroughs.

  Status: 20 decisions, 16 open questions, v0 package shipped and smoke-tested.
  Ready to continue with whatever's next — the natural follow-ups are (a)
  growing the v0 package toward parity with the walkthroughs
  (object/EH/openarray opcodes), (b) starting the m3front MSIR builder so we can
   compile real source, or (c) closing one of the still-open questions (LOCK
  lowering, generics, unsafe).

      143 +        RETURN Text.Equal(a.structName, b.structName);        
      144      | TypeKind.ProcType =>
      145          IF NUMBER(a.procParams^) # NUMBER(b.procParams^) THEN
      146            RETURN FALSE

      179 +PROCEDURE ObjectName(t: T): TEXT = BEGIN RETURN t.structName E
          +ND ObjectName;

          +bjectSuper;                                                   
      181 +PROCEDURE ObjectFieldCount(t: T): INTEGER =                   
      182 +  BEGIN                                                       
      183 +    IF t.fields = NIL THEN RETURN 0 END;                      
      184 +    RETURN NUMBER(t.fields^);                                 
      185 +  END ObjectFieldCount;                                       
      186 +PROCEDURE ObjectField(t: T;  i: INTEGER): Field =             
      187 +  BEGIN RETURN t.fields[i] END ObjectField;                   
      188 +PROCEDURE ObjectMethodCount(t: T): INTEGER =                  
      189 +  BEGIN                                                       
      190 +    IF t.objMethods = NIL THEN RETURN 0 END;                  
      191 +    RETURN NUMBER(t.objMethods^);                             
      192 +  END ObjectMethodCount;                                      
      193 +PROCEDURE ObjectMethod(t: T;  i: INTEGER): Method =           
      194 +  BEGIN RETURN t.objMethods[i] END ObjectMethod;              
      195 +PROCEDURE ObjectDescriptorSym(t: T): TEXT =                   
      196 +  BEGIN RETURN t.objDescriptorSym END ObjectDescriptorSym;    
      197 +                                                              
      198 +(* Look up a method by selector, walking the super chain. *)  
      199 +PROCEDURE ObjectMethodIndex(t: T;  selector: TEXT): INTEGER = 
      200 +  VAR cur := t;  base := 0;                                   
      201 +  BEGIN                                                       
      202 +    (* Walk to root to compute total slot offset, then look in
          + this type's                                                  
      203 +       own methods first. For v0 we use simple unification: ea
          +ch type's                                                     
      204 +       methods are appended; selector resolution scans in decl
          +aration order                                                 
      205 +       through the chain root→leaf, returning the first match.
          + *)                                                           
      206 +    (* Simpler: just scan local methods, then super. Caller ca
          +n rewalk. *)                                                  
      207 +    WHILE cur # NIL DO                                        
      208 +      IF cur.objMethods # NIL THEN                            
      209 +        FOR i := 0 TO LAST(cur.objMethods^) DO                
      210 +          IF Text.Equal(cur.objMethods[i].name, selector) THEN
      211 +            RETURN base + i;                                  
      212 +          END;                                                
      213 +        END;                                                  
      214 +        INC(base, NUMBER(cur.objMethods^));                   
      215 +      END;                                                    
      216 +      cur := cur.objSuper;                                    
      217 +    END;                                                      
      218 +    RETURN -1;                                                
      219 +  END ObjectMethodIndex;                                      
      220 +                                                              
      221 +PROCEDURE ObjectFieldIndex(t: T;  name: TEXT): INTEGER =      
      222 +  VAR cur := t;  base := 0;                                   
      223 +  BEGIN                                                       
      224 +    WHILE cur # NIL DO                                        
      225 +      IF cur.fields # NIL THEN                                
      226 +        FOR i := 0 TO LAST(cur.fields^) DO                    
      227 +          IF Text.Equal(cur.fields[i].name, name) THEN        
      228 +            RETURN base + i;                                  
      229 +          END;                                                
      230 +        END;                                                  
      231 +        INC(base, NUMBER(cur.fields^));                       
      232 +      END;                                                    
      233 +      cur := cur.objSuper;                                    
      234 +    END;                                                      
      235 +    RETURN -1;                                                
      236 +  END ObjectFieldIndex;                                       
      237 +                                                              
      238  (*------------------------------------------------------------
           ----- Values *)
      239  
      240  REVEAL Value = BRANDED "MSIR.Value" REF RECORD

          +lue =

      690 +  END BuildGcStore;

      692 +PROCEDURE objectOrStruct(t: T): T =                           
      693 +  (* Strip a single layer of GcRef/Ptr/GcSlot to expose the un
          +derlying                                                      
      694 +     Object or Struct type for field lookup. *)               
      695 +  BEGIN                                                       
      696 +    CASE Kind(t) OF                                           
      697 +    | TypeKind.GcRef, TypeKind.Ptr, TypeKind.GcSlot => RETURN 
          +EltType(t);                                                   
      698 +    ELSE RETURN t                                             
      699 +    END;                                                      
      700 +  END objectOrStruct;                                         
      701 +                                                              
      702 +PROCEDURE fieldType(objT: T;  name: TEXT): T =                
      703 +  VAR cur := objT;                                            
      704 +  BEGIN                                                       
      705 +    WHILE cur # NIL DO                                        
      706 +      IF cur.fields # NIL THEN                                
      707 +        FOR i := 0 TO LAST(cur.fields^) DO                    
      708 +          IF Text.Equal(cur.fields[i].name, name) THEN        
      709 +            RETURN cur.fields[i].type;                        
      710 +          END;                                                
      711 +        END;                                                  
      712 +      END;                                                    
      713 +      IF Kind(cur) = TypeKind.Object THEN cur := cur.objSuper 
      714 +                                     ELSE cur := NIL END;     
      715 +    END;                                                      
      716 +    RETURN NIL;                                               
      717 +  END fieldType;                                              
      718 +                                                              
      719 +PROCEDURE BuildFieldAddr(b: Block;  name: TEXT;               
      720 +                         obj: Value;  fieldName: TEXT): Value 
          +=                                                             
      721 +  VAR                                                         
      722 +    i := NEW(Insn);                                           
      723 +    ops := NEW(REF ARRAY OF Value, 1);                        
      724 +    objT := objectOrStruct(obj.type);                         
      725 +    ftype := fieldType(objT, fieldName);                      
      726 +  BEGIN                                                       
      727 +    <* ASSERT ftype # NIL, "BuildFieldAddr: field not found" *
          +>                                                             
      728 +    i.op := Op.FieldAddr;                                     
      729 +    i.selector := fieldName;                                  
      730 +    ops[0] := obj;                                            
      731 +    i.operands := ops;                                        
      732 +    i.result := makeResult(b, TPtr(ftype), name, i);          
      733 +    addInsn(b, i);                                            
      734 +    RETURN i.result;                                          
      735 +  END BuildFieldAddr;                                         
      736 +                                                              
      737 +PROCEDURE BuildNew(b: Block;  name: TEXT;  type: T): Value =  
      738 +  VAR i := NEW(Insn);                                         
      739 +  BEGIN                                                       
      740 +    <* ASSERT Kind(type) = TypeKind.Object,                   
      741 +       "BuildNew: type must be Object" *>                     
      742 +    i.op := Op.New;                                           
      743 +    i.targetType := type;                                     
      744 +    i.result := makeResult(b, TGcRef(type), name, i);         
      745 +    addInsn(b, i);                                            
      746 +    RETURN i.result;                                          
      747 +  END BuildNew;                                               
      748 +                                                              
      749 +PROCEDURE BuildDispatch(b: Block;  name: TEXT;                
      750 +                        obj: Value;  methodName: TEXT;        
      751 +                        READONLY args: ARRAY OF Value): Value 
          +=                                                             
      752 +  VAR                                                         
      753 +    i := NEW(Insn);                                           
      754 +    objT := objectOrStruct(obj.type);                         
      755 +    midx: INTEGER;                                            
      756 +    cur: T;                                                   
      757 +    methodSig: T := NIL;                                      
      758 +    resultT: T := NIL;                                        
      759 +    nArgs := NUMBER(args);                                    
      760 +    ops := NEW(REF ARRAY OF Value, 1 + nArgs);                
      761 +  BEGIN                                                       
      762 +    <* ASSERT Kind(objT) = TypeKind.Object,                   
      763 +       "BuildDispatch: object operand must be Object-typed" *>
      764 +    midx := ObjectMethodIndex(objT, methodName);              
      765 +    <* ASSERT midx >= 0, "BuildDispatch: method not found in t
          +ype chain" *>                                                 
      766 +    (* find the actual method record in the chain to get its s
          +ignature *)                                                   
      767 +    cur := objT;                                              
      768 +    WHILE cur # NIL AND methodSig = NIL DO                    
      769 +      IF cur.objMethods # NIL THEN                            
      770 +        FOR k := 0 TO LAST(cur.objMethods^) DO                
      771 +          IF Text.Equal(cur.objMethods[k].name, methodName) TH
          +EN                                                            
      772 +            methodSig := cur.objMethods[k].procType;          
      773 +            EXIT;                                             
      774 +          END;                                                
      775 +        END;                                                  
      776 +      END;                                                    
      777 +      cur := cur.objSuper;                                    
      778 +    END;                                                      
      779 +    <* ASSERT methodSig # NIL *>                              
      780 +    resultT := methodSig.procResult;                          
      781 +                                                              
      782 +    i.op := Op.Dispatch;                                      
      783 +    i.selector := methodName;                                 
      784 +    ops[0] := obj;                                            
      785 +    FOR k := 0 TO nArgs - 1 DO ops[1 + k] := args[k] END;     
      786 +    i.operands := ops;                                        
      787 +    IF Kind(resultT) # TypeKind.Void THEN                     
      788 +      i.result := makeResult(b, resultT, name, i);            
      789 +    END;                                                      
      790 +    addInsn(b, i);                                            
      791 +    RETURN i.result;                                          
      792 +  END BuildDispatch;                                          
      793 +                                                              
      794 +PROCEDURE BuildNarrow(b: Block;  name: TEXT;                  
      795 +                      obj: Value;  targetType: T): Value =    
      796 +  VAR                                                         
      797 +    i := NEW(Insn);                                           
      798 +    ops := NEW(REF ARRAY OF Value, 1);                        
      799 +  BEGIN                                                       
      800 +    i.op := Op.Narrow;                                        
      801 +    i.targetType := targetType;                               
      802 +    ops[0] := obj;                                            
      803 +    i.operands := ops;                                        
      804 +    i.result := makeResult(b, TGcRef(targetType), name, i);   
      805 +    addInsn(b, i);                                            
      806 +    RETURN i.result;                                          
      807 +  END BuildNarrow;                                            
      808 +                                                              
      809 +PROCEDURE BuildIstype(b: Block;  name: TEXT;                  
      810 +                      obj: Value;  targetType: T): Value =    
      811 +  VAR                                                         
      812 +    i := NEW(Insn);                                           
      813 +    ops := NEW(REF ARRAY OF Value, 1);                        
      814 +  BEGIN                                                       
      815 +    i.op := Op.Istype;                                        
      816 +    i.targetType := targetType;                               
      817 +    ops[0] := obj;                                            
      818 +    i.operands := ops;                                        
      819 +    i.result := makeResult(b, TI1(), name, i);                
      820 +    addInsn(b, i);                                            
      821 +    RETURN i.result;                                          
      822 +  END BuildIstype;                                            
      823 +                                                              
      824  BEGIN
      825  END MSIR.

⏺ Both procedures print correctly. The new MaybeArea exercises object types,
  istype, cond_br, narrow, and dispatch end-to-end.

  State of v0+1

  Now in m3-sys/msir:
  - Types: scalars (i1, i8…i64, w8…w64, f32/64/128), Ptr, GcRef, GcSlot, Struct,
   Object (with super, fields, methods, descriptor), ProcType.
  - Ops: load, store, alloca, gc.load, gc.store, field_addr, iadd, isub, imul,
  icmp, br, cond_br, ret, unreachable, call, new, dispatch, narrow, istype.
  - Object queries include super-chain method/field lookup, so dispatch
  correctly resolves area on gc_ref Dog even though Dog inherits it from Animal.

  OBJECT walkthrough parity: reached. The IR can express every op the OBJECT
  walkthrough used.

  Still missing for full walkthrough parity, in roughly priority order:
  1. EH envelopes: try { … } except { … }, try_finally, raise, unwind_to,
  ret_through_envelope. Largest remaining chunk; structural addition (envelopes
  contain blocks).
  2. Open arrays: openarray type kind,
  openarray.size/.data/.elem_addr/.deref/.new, subarray, subscript_check.
  3. Sets and subrange: set<T,lo,hi> / subrange<T,lo,hi> type kinds, set_* ops,
  range_check, nil_check.
  4. Module descriptors and procedure attributes: raises {…}, noreturn, cc,
  personality, linkage; module-level imports/exports; descriptor synthesis.
  5. Verifier (per A9): structural invariants, type consistency, cross-envelope
  rules, dominance, GC contract.

  I'd suggest EH next — it's architecturally distinct (introduces envelope
  nesting in the IR) and unlocks the TRY walkthrough as a typecheck target. Open
   arrays after EH is mostly mechanical (new type kind + ops). Verifier last,
  once we have enough surface to verify against.

  Want me to do EH next?

✻ Baked for 5m 25s

❯ Yes                                                                           

⏺ Designing EH for v0+EH:
  - Envelopes (try/try_finally) are RegionItem alongside Blocks at the procedure
   level.
  - Body / handlers / finally are single blocks for v0+EH (regions come later
  when motivated).
  - New ops: raise, unwind_to, ret_through_envelope, invoke (call from inside a
  try).

      410 +PROCEDURE NewTryExcept(body: Block;                           
      411 +                       READONLY handlers: ARRAY OF Handler): E
          +nvelope =

      146 +  VAR

      149 +    (* External Lookup procedure (declaration only). *)

      152 +        ARRAY OF MSIR.Param { MSIR.Param{"key", i64, MSIR.Para
          +mMode.ByValue} },                                             
      153 +        i64);

      156 +        "@WithLog",                                           
      157 +        ARRAY OF MSIR.Param { MSIR.Param{"key", i64, MSIR.Para
          +mMode.ByValue} },                                             
      158 +        i64);                                                 
      159 +    key   := MSIR.ProcParam(p, 0);                            
      160 +                                                              
      161 +    entry := MSIR.NewBlock("entry", ARRAY OF MSIR.BlockParam{}
          +);                                                            
      162 +    body  := MSIR.NewBlock("body",  ARRAY OF MSIR.BlockParam{}
          +);

          +);                                                            
      164 +    h2    := MSIR.NewBlock("h2",                              
      165 +                ARRAY OF MSIR.BlockParam {                    
      166 +                  MSIR.BlockParam{"msg", i64}                 
      167 +                });                                           
      168 +    merge := MSIR.NewBlock("merge",                           
      169 +                ARRAY OF MSIR.BlockParam {                    
      170 +                  MSIR.BlockParam{"n", i64}                   
      171 +                });                                           
      172 +                                                              
      173 +    v       : MSIR.Value;                                     
      174 +    n       : MSIR.Value;                                     
      175 +    negOne  := MSIR.ConstInt(i64, -1L);                       
      176 +    negTwo  := MSIR.ConstInt(i64, -2L);                       
      177 +    env     : MSIR.Envelope;

      179 +    (* entry: br body — entering the try envelope *)          
      180 +    MSIR.BuildBr(entry, body, ARRAY OF MSIR.Value{});         
      181 +                                                              
      182 +    (* body: %v = invoke @Lookup(key); unwind_to merge(%v) *) 
      183 +    v := MSIR.BuildInvoke(body, "v", lookup, ARRAY OF MSIR.Val
          +ue{key});

      185 +                                                              
      186 +    (* h1 (NotFound): unwind_to merge(-1) *)                  
      187 +    MSIR.BuildUnwindTo(h1, merge, ARRAY OF MSIR.Value{negOne})
          +;                                                             
      188 +                                                              
      189 +    (* h2 (Bad(msg)): unwind_to merge(-2) *)                  
      190 +    MSIR.BuildUnwindTo(h2, merge, ARRAY OF MSIR.Value{negTwo})
          +;                                                             
      191 +                                                              
      192 +    (* merge: ret %n *)                                       
      193 +    n := MSIR.BlockParamValue(merge, 0);                      
      194 +    MSIR.BuildRet(merge, n);                                  
      195 +                                                              
      196 +    env := MSIR.NewTryExcept(body,                            
      197 +        ARRAY OF MSIR.Handler {

      199 +          MSIR.Handler{"@Bad",      h2}                       
      200 +        });                                                   
      201 +                                                              
      202 +    MSIR.ProcAddBlock(p, entry);                              
      203 +    MSIR.ProcAddEnvelope(p, env);                             
      204 +    MSIR.ProcAddBlock(p, merge);                              
      205 +

⏺ EH works. The output shows all three procedures, with the try/except envelope
  correctly nested between the procedure-level entry and merge blocks. invoke,
  unwind_to, parameterized handler blocks, and exception identity symbols all
  print correctly.

  State after v0+EH

  Now in m3-sys/msir:
  - All v0+1 features (objects, RTTI, allocas, GC slot ops, fields,
  dispatch/narrow/istype).
  - Envelopes: try and try_finally constructors, single-block
  bodies/handlers/finally for v0.
  - EH ops: invoke, raise, unwind_to, ret_through_envelope.
  - Procedure region: items list mixing blocks and envelopes; printer iterates
  in order.

  Walkthrough parity:
  - ✅ OBJECT + METHOD
  - ✅ TRY/EXCEPT/FINALLY (try_finally constructor exists; only try/except
  exercised in test — try_finally constructor works but isn't built by the smoke
   test yet)
  - ❌ Open arrays
  - ❌ Module init / globals
  - ❌ Sets, subrange, range_check
  - ❌ NARROW/TYPECASE/ISTYPE — narrow and istype are in; typecase still missing
   as a structured op
  - ❌ Verifier

  Limitations of v0+EH:
  - Envelope bodies, handlers, and finally are single blocks. Multi-block bodies
   (which the walkthroughs hint at but don't strictly require) need a recursive
  Region structure — defer to v0+EH-2 if motivated.
  - No verifier enforcement of cross-envelope rules; honor system for now.
  - Finally blocks need an end_finally terminator op when we add lowering —
  currently they end with whatever the user puts (typically nothing, which the
  printer handles gracefully).

  Given the scope, I'd suggest open arrays next — it adds one type kind and a
  small set of intrinsics, no new structural shapes. Then
  sets/subrange/range_check, then typecase as a structured op, then the
  verifier, then we'd be at full walkthrough parity.

  Want me to push on with open arrays?

✻ Crunched for 7m 52s

  that's part of the broader allocation work.

       979 +PROCEDURE BuildOpenArraySize(b: Block;  name: TEXT;          
       980 +                             oa: Value;  dim: INTEGER): Value
           + =                                                           
       981 +  VAR                                                        
       982 +    i := NEW(Insn);

       984 +    dimVal := ConstInt(TI(64), VAL(dim, LONGINT));           
       985 +  BEGIN                                                      
       986 +    <* ASSERT Kind(oa.type) = TypeKind.OpenArray,            
       987 +       "BuildOpenArraySize: operand must be openarray" *>    
       988 +    <* ASSERT 0 <= dim AND dim < oa.type.openArrayRank,      
       989 +       "BuildOpenArraySize: dim out of range" *>             
       990 +    i.op := Op.OpenArraySize;                                
       991 +    ops[0] := oa;                                            
       992 +    ops[1] := dimVal;                                        
       993 +    i.operands := ops;                                       
       994 +    i.result := makeResult(b, TI(64), name, i);              
       995 +    addInsn(b, i);                                           
       996 +    RETURN i.result;                                         
       997 +  END BuildOpenArraySize;                                    
       998 +                                                             
       999 +PROCEDURE BuildOpenArrayElemAddr(b: Block;  name: TEXT;      
      1000 +                                 oa: Value;                  
      1001 +                                 READONLY indices: ARRAY OF V
           +alue): Value =                                               
      1002 +  VAR                                                        
      1003 +    i := NEW(Insn);                                          
      1004 +    nIdx := NUMBER(indices);                                 
      1005 +    ops := NEW(REF ARRAY OF Value, 1 + nIdx);                
      1006 +  BEGIN                                                      
      1007 +    <* ASSERT Kind(oa.type) = TypeKind.OpenArray,            
      1008 +       "BuildOpenArrayElemAddr: operand must be openarray" *>
      1009 +    <* ASSERT nIdx = oa.type.openArrayRank,                  
      1010 +       "BuildOpenArrayElemAddr: index count must equal rank" 
           +*>                                                           
      1011 +    i.op := Op.OpenArrayElemAddr;                            
      1012 +    ops[0] := oa;                                            
      1013 +    FOR k := 0 TO nIdx - 1 DO ops[1 + k] := indices[k] END;  
      1014 +    i.operands := ops;                                       
      1015 +    i.result := makeResult(b, TPtr(oa.type.elt), name, i);   
      1016 +    addInsn(b, i);                                           
      1017 +    RETURN i.result;                                         
      1018 +  END BuildOpenArrayElemAddr;                                
      1019 +                                                             
      1020 +PROCEDURE BuildSubarray(b: Block;  name: TEXT;               
      1021 +                        oa: Value;  lo, count: Value): Value 
           +=                                                            
      1022 +  VAR

      1027 +       "BuildSubarray: operand must be openarray" *>         
      1028 +    <* ASSERT oa.type.openArrayRank = 1,

      209 +PROCEDURE BuildOpenArraySum(m: MSIR.Module) =                 
      210 +  (* PROCEDURE Sum(a: ARRAY OF INTEGER): INTEGER =            
      211 +       VAR s := 0;                                            
      212 +       BEGIN                                                  
      213 +         FOR i := 0 TO NUMBER(a) - 1 DO s := s + a[i] END;    
      214 +         RETURN s;                                            
      215 +       END Sum; *)                                            
      216 +  VAR                                                         
      217 +    i64    := MSIR.TI(64);                                    
      218 +    oa1i64 := MSIR.TOpenArray(1, i64);                        
      219 +                                                              
      220 +    p := MSIR.NewProc(                                        
      221 +        "@Sum",                                               
      222 +        ARRAY OF MSIR.Param { MSIR.Param{"a", oa1i64, MSIR.Par
          +amMode.ByValue} },                                            
      223 +        i64);                                                 
      224 +    a := MSIR.ProcParam(p, 0);                                
      225 +                                                              
      226 +    entry := MSIR.NewBlock("entry", ARRAY OF MSIR.BlockParam{}
          +);

      228 +        ARRAY OF MSIR.BlockParam {                            
      229 +          MSIR.BlockParam{"i", i64},                          
      230 +          MSIR.BlockParam{"s", i64}                           
      231 +        });                                                   
      232 +    body  := MSIR.NewBlock("body",                            
      233 +        ARRAY OF MSIR.BlockParam {                            
      234 +          MSIR.BlockParam{"i", i64},                          
      235 +          MSIR.BlockParam{"s", i64}                           
      236 +        });                                                   
      237 +    exit  := MSIR.NewBlock("exit",                            
      238 +        ARRAY OF MSIR.BlockParam {                            
      239 +          MSIR.BlockParam{"result", i64}                      
      240 +        });                                                   
      241 +                                                              
      242 +    n, zero, one : MSIR.Value;                                
      243 +    bi, bs, ep, v, s2, i2, lt, i_, s_ : MSIR.Value;           
      244 +  BEGIN                                                       
      245 +    MSIR.ProcAddBlock(p, entry);                              
      246 +    MSIR.ProcAddBlock(p, loop);                               
      247 +    MSIR.ProcAddBlock(p, body);                               
      248 +    MSIR.ProcAddBlock(p, exit);                               
      249 +                                                              
      250 +    zero := MSIR.ConstInt(i64, 0L);                           
      251 +    one  := MSIR.ConstInt(i64, 1L);                           
      252 +                                                              
      253 +    (* entry: %n = openarray.size a, 0; br loop(0, 0) *)      
      254 +    n := MSIR.BuildOpenArraySize(entry, "n", a, 0);           
      255 +    MSIR.BuildBr(entry, loop, ARRAY OF MSIR.Value{zero, zero})
          +;                                                             
      256 +                                                              
      257 +    (* loop(%i, %s):                                          
      258 +         %lt = icmp slt %i, %n                                
      259 +         cond_br %lt, body(%i, %s), exit(%s) *)               
      260 +    i_ := MSIR.BlockParamValue(loop, 0);                      
      261 +    s_ := MSIR.BlockParamValue(loop, 1);                      
      262 +    lt := MSIR.BuildICmp(loop, "lt", MSIR.CmpPred.Slt, i_, n);
      263 +    MSIR.BuildCondBr(loop, lt,                                
      264 +        body, ARRAY OF MSIR.Value{i_, s_},                    
      265 +        exit, ARRAY OF MSIR.Value{s_});                       
      266 +                                                              
      267 +    (* body(%i, %s):                                          
      268 +         subscript_check %i, %n                               
      269 +         %ep = openarray.elem_addr a, %i                      
      270 +         %v  = load i64, %ep                                  
      271 +         %s2 = iadd %s, %v                                    
      272 +         %i2 = iadd %i, 1                                     
      273 +         br loop(%i2, %s2) *)                                 
      274 +    bi := MSIR.BlockParamValue(body, 0);                      
      275 +    bs := MSIR.BlockParamValue(body, 1);                      
      276 +    MSIR.BuildSubscriptCheck(body, bi, n);                    
      277 +    ep := MSIR.BuildOpenArrayElemAddr(body, "ep", a, ARRAY OF 
          +MSIR.Value{bi});                                              
      278 +    v  := MSIR.BuildLoad(body, "v", i64, ep);                 
      279 +    s2 := MSIR.BuildIAdd(body, "s2", bs, v);                  
      280 +    i2 := MSIR.BuildIAdd(body, "i2", bi, one);                
      281 +    MSIR.BuildBr(body, loop, ARRAY OF MSIR.Value{i2, s2});    
      282 +                                                              
      283 +    (* exit(%result): ret %result *)                          
      284 +    MSIR.BuildRet(exit, MSIR.BlockParamValue(exit, 0));       
      285 +                                                              
      286 +    MSIR.ModuleAddProc(m, p);                                 
      287 +  END BuildOpenArraySum;                                      
      288 +

⏺ Open-array Sum prints exactly as the walkthrough designed: openarray<1> i64
  parameter, openarray.size a, 0, the loop with block-parameter SSA,

  State after v0+OpenArray

  Now in m3-sys/msir:
  - All v0+EH features.
  - Open-array type kind with rank parameter; type printed as openarray<rank> 
  elt.
  - Open-array ops: openarray.size, openarray.elem_addr, subarray,
  subscript_check, nil_check.

  Walkthrough parity:
  - ✅ OBJECT + METHOD
  - ✅ TRY/EXCEPT (try_finally constructor exists; not yet exercised in test)
  - ✅ Open arrays (1D parameter + bounds-checked iteration)
  - ❌ openarray.new / openarray.deref / heap REF ARRAY OF — defer with module
  init
  - ❌ Module init / globals / module descriptor
  - ❌ Sets + subrange + range_check
  - ❌ TYPECASE as a structured op
  - ❌ Bitfields
  - ❌ Procedure attributes (raises {}, noreturn, etc.)
  - ❌ Verifier

  Natural next chunks (in roughly increasing scope):
  1. Sets + subrange + range_check — small. New type kinds (set<T,lo,hi>,
  subrange<T,lo,hi>); set ops as intrinsics; range_check. Parallel structure to
  opens.
  2. TYPECASE as a composite op — modest. New op carrying clause list with
  parameterized handler blocks (like try-except).
  3. Procedure attributes — small. raises {…}, noreturn, cc, personality as
  attributes on Proc.
  4. Module descriptors and heap allocation — medium. openarray.new,
  openarray.deref, module imports/exports/globals/descriptor synthesis.
  5. Verifier — large. Per A9, all the structural/type/dominance/GC checks.

  I'd suggest sets+subrange+range_check next — smallest piece, parallels the
  open-array shape. Then TYPECASE (rounds out the "intrinsic surface" decision
  D18). Then procedure attributes. Then heap allocation + module work as a
  single coherent chunk. Verifier last.

  Push on with sets/subrange?

✻ Cooked for 3m 54s

  State after v0+Sets

  Now in m3-sys/msir:
  - All v0+OpenArray features.
  - Subrange and Set type kinds with bounds.
  - Convert (representation conversion); RangeCheck (explicit range check op).
  - Set ops: union, intersect, difference, member.

  Walkthrough parity:
  - ✅ OBJECT + METHOD
  - ✅ TRY/EXCEPT
  - ✅ Open arrays (1D, parameter-shaped)
  - ✅ Sets + subrange + range_check
  - ❌ TYPECASE as a structured op (narrow/istype are in)
  - ❌ Module init / globals / heap REF ARRAY OF
  - ❌ Procedure attributes (raises, noreturn, etc.)
  - ❌ Bitfields (small, deferred)
  - ❌ Verifier

  Remaining chunks in roughly increasing scope:
  1. TYPECASE as a composite op — small. Completes D18's intrinsic surface;
  structurally similar to try-except (clause list + parameterized blocks).
  2. Procedure attributes — small. raises {…}, noreturn, cc, personality,
  linkage as attributes on Proc.
  3. Module-level globals + heap allocation — medium. gc_slot globals,
  openarray.new, openarray.deref, module imports, module descriptors per D14.
  4. Verifier — large. Per A9.

  I'd suggest TYPECASE next — smallest piece, completes the RTTI intrinsic
  surface, structurally similar to try-except so reuses existing patterns. Then
  procedure attributes (also small). Then the module/heap chunk as a unit since
  it's all interrelated. Then verifier.

  Push on with TYPECASE?

      1273 +                            elt: T;                          
      1274 +                            READONLY sizes: ARRAY OF Value): 
           +Value =                                                      
      1275 +  VAR

      1277 +    n    := NUMBER(sizes);                                   
      1278 +    ops  := NEW(REF ARRAY OF Value, n);                      
      1279 +    heap := THeapArray(n, elt);                              
      1280 +  BEGIN                                                      
      1281 +    <* ASSERT n >= 1, "BuildOpenArrayNew: at least one size r
           +equired" *>                                                  
      1282 +    i.op := Op.OpenArrayNew;                                 
      1283 +    i.targetType := elt;                 (* element type for 
           +printer *)                                                   
      1284 +    FOR k := 0 TO n - 1 DO ops[k] := sizes[k] END;           
      1285 +    i.operands := ops;

      1291 +PROCEDURE BuildOpenArrayDeref(b: Block;  name: TEXT;  ref: Va
           +lue): Value =

      1301 +       "BuildOpenArrayDeref: operand must be gc_ref to a heap
           + array" *>

      1303 +    i.op := Op.OpenArrayDeref;

      1305 +    i.operands := ops;
