      373 +        Wr.PutText(wr, Fmt.Int(nOps));                        
      374 +        Wr.PutText(wr, ">");                                  
      375 +        FOR k := 0 TO nOps - 1 DO                             
      376 +          Wr.PutText(wr, ", ");                               
      377 +          NameRef(wr, MSIR.InsnOperand(i, k));                
      378 +        END;                                                  
      379 +    | MSIR.Op.OpenArrayDeref =>                               
      380 +        Wr.PutText(wr, " ");                                  
      381 +        NameRef(wr, MSIR.InsnOperand(i, 0));                  
      382      END;
      383      Wr.PutText(wr, "\n");
      384    END Insn;

  State after v0+Module

  Now in m3-sys/msir:
  - All previous features.
  - HeapArray type kind (heap_array<rank> elt).
  - openarray.new (heap allocation) and openarray.deref (heap REF → fat
  pointer).
  - Module-level globals as first-class entities, with addressable Value handles
   for use in load/store/gc.load/gc.store.
  - Globals printed at the module scope.

  Walkthrough parity:
  - ✅ OBJECT + METHOD
  - ✅ TRY / EXCEPT
  - ✅ Open arrays (1D parameter + heap allocation/deref)
  - ✅ Sets + subrange + range_check
  - ✅ NARROW / TYPECASE / ISTYPE
  - ✅ Procedure attributes
  - ✅ Module-level globals + heap allocation
  - ❌ Bitfields (small, deferred)
  - ❌ Verifier (large; A9 invariants)

  (a) is essentially done. The remaining gap before full walkthrough parity is
  the verifier — none of our walkthroughs really exercise it visibly, but it's
  what makes the IR enforceable rather than just suggestive.

  Two natural next paths:
  - Verifier: completes (a) properly. Per A9: structural op invariants (typecase
   exhaustiveness, try has handlers, gc.load operand kind, etc.), type
  consistency, cross-envelope rules, SSA dominance, GC contract,
  procedure-attribute consistency. Significant chunk of code, but every check is
   small and self-contained.
  - Move to (b): starting the m3front MSIR builder — wire it into the actual
  compiler so we can compile real source. The IR is now expressive enough to
  attempt this on a tiny subset (procedures with integer arithmetic, no
  objects/EH yet).

  Which direction?

⏺ Verifier integrated and passes all 10 test procedures. v0 verifier catches:
  - Branch target arity and argument type mismatches
  - Cond_br condition is i1
  - Binary op operand type mismatches; ICmp result is i1
  - gc.load operand is gc_slot; gc.store argument shape
  - Call/invoke arg count matches callee params
  - Dispatch selector exists in object's super chain
  - Typecase has mandatory ELSE clause and non-NIL clause blocks
  - Try-except has at least one handler; try-finally has finally block
  - Ret value type matches procedure result type
  - Block ends with a terminator; terminator only at block end
  - Zero-block procedures treated as forward declarations

  State after v0+Verifier — and (a) is done

  Now in m3-sys/msir:
  - All previous features.
  - Verifier with op-level local checks per A9.

  Walkthrough parity — full:
  - ✅ OBJECT + METHOD
  - ✅ TRY / EXCEPT
  - ✅ Open arrays (parameter, heap allocation, deref)
  - ✅ Sets + subrange + range_check
  - ✅ NARROW / TYPECASE / ISTYPE
  - ✅ Procedure attributes
  - ✅ Module-level globals + heap allocation
  - ✅ Verifier (local checks)
  - ❌ Bitfields — small, deferred; the only missing IR feature

  Verifier follow-ups deferred:
  - SSA dominance analysis (requires CFG building).
  - Cross-envelope control-flow rules per D8 (requires nesting tracking).
  - GC contract checks beyond local op signatures (addrspace propagation through
   GEP chains).
  - Exhaustive procedure-attribute consistency (e.g. confirming personality
  presence whenever try is used).

  Plan (a) — grow v0 toward walkthrough parity — is essentially complete. The
  package can express every IR feature the walkthroughs use (modulo bitfields
  and the deferred verifier checks), and the smoke test exercises the major
  surfaces.

  Next is (b) — start the m3front MSIR builder. This is where the IR meets the
  actual compiler. The shape is: take a small subset of m3front (probably just
  procedure compilation with integer arithmetic, no objects/EH yet) and make it
  emit MSIR alongside its existing M3CG calls. Diff-test against the OBJECT and
  TRY walkthroughs would come later as the subset grows.

  Want to start (b)?
